24:B2 | 41:Enrollment by Racial/Ethnic Category.
41:Provide numbers of undergraduate students for each of the following categories as of the institution’s official fall reporting date or as of October 15, 2025.
41:• | 54:Include international students only in the category "Nonresidents."
41:• | 54:Complete the “Total Undergraduates” column only if you cannot provide data for the first two columns.
41:• | 54:Report as your institution reports to IPEDS: persons who are Hispanic should be reported only on the Hispanic line,
41:not under any race, and persons who are non-Hispanic multi-racial should be reported only under "Two or more races."
41:• | 54:New guidance from IPEDS for reporting aggregate data:
41:Racial/ethnic designations are requested only for United States citizens, residents, and other eligible non-citizens.
41:Eligible non-citizens include all students who completed high school or a GED equivalency within the United States (including DACA and undocumented students)
41:and who were not on an F-1 non-immigrant student visa at the time of high school graduation.
23:CDS-B | 540:Page 5
245:Common Data Set 2025-2026
41:More information about other eligible (for financial aid purposes) non-citizens is available at:
41:https://studentaid.gov/understandaid/eligibility/requirements/non-us-citizens.
41:Nonresident - A person who is not a citizen or national of the United States and who is in this country on a student visaor temporary
41:basis and does not have the right to remain indefinitely. Do not include DACA, undocumented, or othereligible noncitizens in this category.
41:NOTE - Nonresidents are to be reported separately, in the boxes provided, rather than included in any of the
41:seven racial/ethnic categories or in race/ethnicity unknown.

42:fall enrollment.

237:H. FINANCIAL AID
48:Please refer to the following financial aid definitions when completing Section H.
48:Awarded aid: The dollar amounts offered to financial aid applicants.
48:Financial aid applicant: Any applicant who submits any one of the institutionally required financial aid applications/forms, such as
48:the FAFSA.
48:Indebtedness: Aggregate dollar amount borrowed through any loan program (federal, state, subsidized, unsubsidized, private,
48:etc.; excluding parent loans) while the student was enrolled at an institution. Student loans co-signed by a parent are assumed to
48:be the responsibility of the student and should be included.
48:Institutional scholarships and grants: Endowed scholarships, annual gifts and tuition funded grants for which the institution
48:determines the recipient.
48:Financial need: As determined by your institution using the federal methodology and/or your institution's own standards.
48:Need-based aid: College-funded or college-administered award from institutional, state, federal, or other sources for which a
48:student must have financial need to qualify. This includes both institutional and non-institutional student aid (grants, jobs, and
48:loans).
48:Need-based scholarship or grant aid: Scholarships and grants from institutional, state, federal, or other sources for which a
48:student must have financial need to qualify.
48:Need-based self-help aid: Loans and jobs from institutional, state, federal, or other sources for which a student must
48:demonstrate financial need to qualify.
48:Non-need-based scholarship or grant aid: Scholarships and grants, gifts, or merit-based aid from institutional, state, federal, or
48:other sources (including unrestricted funds or gifts and endowment income) awarded solely on the basis of academic
48:achievement, merit, or any other non-need-based reason. When reporting questions H1 and H2, non-need-based aid that is
48:used to meet need should be counted as need-based aid.
48:Note: Suggested order of precedence for counting non-need money as need-based:
60:1. Non-need institutional grants | 266:6. Non-need outside grants
60:2. Non-need tuition waivers | 266:7. Non-need student loans
60:3. Non-need athletic awards | 266:8. Non-need parent loans
60:4. Non-need federal grants | 266:9. Non-need work
60:5. Non-need state grants
48:Non-need-based self-help aid: Loans and jobs from institutional, state, or other sources for which a student need not
48:demonstrate financial need to qualify.
48:Private student loans: A nonfederal loan made by a lender such as a bank, credit union or private lender used to pay for up to the
48:annual cost of education, less any financial aid received.
48:External scholarships and grants: Scholarships and grants received from outside (private) sources that students bring with them
48:(e.g., Kiwanis, National Merit scholarships). The institution may process paperwork to receive the dollars, but it has no role in
48:determining the recipient or the dollar amount awarded.
48:Work study and employment: Federal and state work study aid, and any employment packaged by your institution in financial aid
48:awards.

63:use the 2024-2025 academic year's CDS Question $B$1 cohort.
48:• | 64:Include aid awarded to international students (i.e., those not qualifying for federal aid).

48:• | 64:Aid that is non-need-based but that was used to meet need should be reported in the need-based aid
63:column.
48:• | 64:For a suggested order of precedence in assigning categories of aid to cover need, see the entry for “non-need-based
48:scholarship or grant aid” on the last page of the definitions section.

46:Please report the number of instructional faculty members in each category for Fall 2025. Include
24:I-1.
46:faculty who are on your institution’s payroll on the census date your institution uses for IPEDS/AAUP.
46:The following definition of full-time instructional faculty is used by the American Association of University Professors
46:(AAUP) in its annual Faculty Compensation Survey (the part time definitions are not used by AAUP). Instructional Faculty
46:is defined as those members of the instructional-research staff whose major regular assignment is instruction, including
46:those with released time for research. Use the chart below to determine inclusions and exclusions:

46:Full-time instructional faculty: faculty employed on a full-time basis for instruction (including those with released time for
46:research)
46:Part-time instructional faculty: Adjuncts and other instructors being paid solely for part-time classroom instruction. Also
46:includes full-time faculty teaching less than two semesters, three quarters, two trimesters, or two four-month sessions.
46:Employees who are not considered full-time instruction faculty but who teach one or more non-clinical credit courses
46:may be counted as part-time faculty.
46:Minority faculty: includes faculty who designate themselves as Black, non-Hispanic; American Indian or Alaska Native;
46:Asian, Native Hawaiian or other Pacific Islander, or Hispanic.
46:Doctorate: includes such degrees as Doctor of Philosophy, Doctor of Education, Doctor of Juridical Science, and Doctor
46:of Public Health in any field such as arts, sciences, education, engineering, business, and public administration. Also
46:includes terminal degrees formerly designated as “first professional,” including dentistry (DDS or DMD), medicine (MD),
46:optometry (OD), osteopathic medicine (DO), pharmacy (DPharm or BPharm), podiatric medicine (DPM), veterinary
46:medicine (DVM), chiropractic (DC or DCM), or law (JD).
46:Terminal master’s degree: a master’s degree that is considered the highest degree in a field: example, M. Arch (in
46:architecture) and MFA (master of fine arts in art or theater).

24:I-3. | 46:Undergraduate Class Size
46:In the table below, please use the following definitions to report information about the size of classes and class
46:sections offered in the Fall 2025 term.
46:Class Sections: A class section is an organized course offered for credit, identified by discipline and
46:number, meeting at a stated time or times in a classroom or similar setting, and not a subsection
46:such as a laboratory or discussion session. Undergraduate class sections are defined as any
46:sections in which at least one degree-seeking undergraduate student is enrolled for credit. Exclude
46:distance learning classes and noncredit classes and individual instruction such as dissertation or
46:thesis research, music instruction, or one-to-one readings. Exclude students in independent study,
46:co-operative programs, internships, foreign language taped tutor sessions, practicums, and all
46:students in one-on-one classes. Each class section should be counted only once and should not be
46:duplicated because of course catalog cross-listings.
46:Class Subsections: A class subsection includes any subsection of a course, such as laboratory,
46:recitation, and discussion subsections that are supplementary in nature and are scheduled to meet
46:separately from the lecture portion of the course. Undergraduate subsections are defined as any
46:subsections of courses in which degree-seeking undergraduate students enrolled for credit. As
46:above, exclude noncredit classes and individual instruction such as dissertation or thesis research,
46:music instruction, or one-to-one readings. Each class subsection should be counted only once and
46:should not be duplicated because of cross-listings.
46:Using the above definitions, please report for each of the following class-size intervals the number of class
46:sections and class subsections offered in Fall 2025. For example, a lecture class with 800 students who met
46:at another time in 40 separate labs with 20 students should be counted once in the “100+” column in the class
46:section column and 40 times under the “20-29” column of the class subsections table.
